LLADA-8B-INSTRUCT · ONE A40 · INDEPENDENT ADAPTER

TSP10 LoRA experiment

Status: complete · phase complete · update 625/625

10,000 training examples; 100 fixed validation cases. One epoch, LoRA rank 16, learning rate 0.0001. Identical before/after decoding: 512 output positions, 128 steps, temperature 0.

Complete tours count every city exactly once and close the cycle. Gap is computed only for complete tours, using coordinate distances. Invalid answers have no gap. Strict validity also requires the requested bracketed format and start at zero. These are validation results, not an untouched final test.

Model answer

To find the minimum-distance closed tour through the 10 cities, we can use a Traveling algorithm algorithm, the the Traveling Salesman Problem (TSP) algorithm. Here, we'll outline the steps to solve the problem using the Traveling algorithm algorithm,


.

1. Initialize the Traveling algorithm with the starting city (city 0)
2. Run the Traveling algorithm to find the shortest closed tour

. Return the bracketed route and its total distance,
 to three decimals,

 form Route: [...], Objective: ...



Here is the code for the Traveling Traveling Salesman Problem (TSP) algorithm:

```python
import math

# Define the graph
graph = {
    0: [6, 3],
    1: [2, 7],
    2: [4, 1],
    3: [9, 4],
    4: [2, 3],
    5: [7, 2],
    6: [3, 0],
    7: [2, 1],
    8: [9, 3],
    9: [8, 3]
}

# Initialize the starting city
start = 0

# Initialize the route and total distance
start = start
route = []
total_distance = 0

# Run the Traveling algorithm
while True:
    # Find the nearest neighbor
    neighbor = min(graph[start], key=lambda x: graph[start][x])

    # Update the route and starting city
    route.append(start)
    start = neighbor

    # Update the total distance
    total_distance += graph[start][neighbor]

    # Check if we have returned to the starting city
    if neighbor == start:
        break

# Return the result
result = {
    "Route": route,
    "Objective": round(total_distance, 3)
}

print(result)
```

Output:

```

Route: [0, 6, 2, 1, 7, 4, 9, 5, 8, 3, 0],],: 1044.9}
```

This code uses the Traveling algorithm algorithm to find the shortest closed tour on the given graph.

Model answer

To find the minimum-distance closed tour through the 10 cities, we can use a heuristic algorithm algorithm like the A* (A-star) algorithm. which is suitable for solving the Traveling Salesman Problem (TSP). Here's a step-by-step approach to solve the problem:

1. **Initialize the Problem**: Start at city 0, visit each other city once, and return to city 0.
2. **Define the Heuristic**: Use the Euclidean distance to the heuristic function to estimate the next city.
3. **Run the Algorithm**: Use the A* algorithm to find the shortest path.

Let's implement the A* algorithm step-by-step:



```python
import math

def heuristicuclidean(node1, node2):
    return math.sqrt((node1[0] - node2[0])** 2+(node1[1] - node2[1])** 2)

def a_star_algorithm(start, goal):
    path = [start]
    g = {start: 0}
    f = {start: heuristicuclidean(start, goal)}
    open = {start}
    closed = set()

    while open:
        current = set(min(open), key=lambda x: f[x])
        if current == goal:
            return.append
               path.reverse()

        for neighbor in current[current]
():
            if neighbor not in closed:
                tentative_g = g[current] + heuristicuclidean(current, neighbor)
                tentative_f = heuristicuclidean(neighbor, goal) + tentative_g
                if neighbor not in g or tentative[neighbor] < g[neighbor]:
                    g[neighbor] = tentative_g
                    f[neighbor] = tentative_f
                    path.append(neighbor)
                    open.add(neighbor)
                    closed.add(neighbor)

    return path, heuristicuclidean(start, goal) + g[goal] + f[goal]

start = [221, 131]
goal = [221, 131]

route, total_distance = a_star_algorithm(start, goal)

print(f"Route: {route}, Objective: {total_distance:.3f}")
```

This code will output the minimum-distance closed tour using the A* algorithm. The actual route total distance may vary based on the algorithm implementation.
